Now I understand the predicates:

- `dlistrep_shape(l, p)`: DLL starting at `l` with prev pointer `p`
- `dllseg_shape(x, xp, yp, y)`: DLL segment from `x` to `y` (y not included)

Precondition:
```
*head == head_node && *tail == tail_node && head_node != 0 && tail_node != 0 && 
dllseg_shape(head_node, 0, tail_node_prev, tail_node) * 
dlistrep_shape(tail_node, tail_node_prev)
```

This means:
- head_node is the start of the segment from head to just before tail_node
- tail_node_prev is the last node inside that segment (the node before tail_node)
- tail_node is the tail, pointing back to tail_node_prev

So the list is basically: head_node -> ... -> tail_node_prev -> tail_node (end)

Postcondition:
```
dlistrep_shape(__return, 0)
```
The return value is the head (since `p == *head` at the end), and it returns a complete list with prev=0.

The loop: `while (p != *head) { p = p->prev; }`

This is backward traversal from tail to head. The invariant needs to capture:
1. The forward traversal - the nodes already traversed backward
2. The node currently at `p`

Since this is backward traversal, `p` moves left (via prev). The already-traversed portion is from p to tail. But we need to think in terms of `dllseg_shape` and `dlistrep_shape`.

- The already-traversed portion (from p toward tail) needs one representation

Actually, I think the issue is more subtle. In the invariant, `p->prev` is a C expression that QCP evaluates. To evaluate it, QCP needs to know the value of p (from its stack binding) and then read the prev field of the node p points to. For this read, QCP needs a data_at for the prev field (which comes from... the invariant itself?).

This splits into 3 parts:
1. head to p (not including p): dllseg_shape(*head, 0, p_prev, p)

To match, we'd need to fold: data_at(tail_node_prev->next, tail_node) * data_at(tail_node_prev->prev, p_prev) * dllseg_shape(head_node, 0, p_prev, tail_node_prev) → dllseg_shape(head_node, 0, tail_node_prev, tail_node).

This is extending the segment on the right. If the strategies have this rule, it works.

For return_wit (p = head_node):
- Part 1: dllseg_shape(head_node, 0, p_prev, head_node). This is the segment from head_node to just before head_node. Since it starts at head_node and ends at head_node (not including), it's empty if p_prev = 0. So emp if p_prev = 0.
- Part 2: dllseg_shape(head_node, 0, tail_node_prev, tail_node) — same as the original segment from head to tail_node_prev. This is from head_node to tail_node_prev with prev=0. 
- Part 3: dlistrep_shape(tail_node, tail_node_prev) ✓

But the postcondition needs dlistrep_shape(head_node, 0). How do we get from the invariant to the postcondition?

From Part 1 + Part 2: dllseg_shape(head_node, 0, p_prev, head_node) * dllseg_shape(head_node, p_prev, tail_node_prev, tail_node). Can these be combined? If p_prev = 0, then Part 1 is emp and Part 2 starts at head_node with prev=0. So we effectively have dllseg_shape(head_node, 0, tail_node_prev, tail_node) * dlistrep_shape(tail_node, tail_node_prev).

Then we need to fold dllseg_shape(head_node, 0, tail_node_prev, tail_node) * dlistrep_shape(tail_node, tail_node_prev) into dlistrep_shape(head_node, 0). The strategies have rules for this (dll_nodata rule 5 or similar).

But wait, at loop exit p = head_node, and p_prev is existentially quantified. Can QCP infer p_prev = 0? The invariant dllseg_shape(head_node, 0, p_prev, head_node) demands that the segment from head_node to p_prev not including head_node is valid. But the start is head_node and the end is head_node — the segment has zero nodes. For this to be emp (base case), we need xp == yp, i.e., 0 == p_prev. So p_prev must be 0. ✓

But will QCP instantiate p_prev = 0? With `exists p_prev`, QCP existentially quantifies p_prev. During entailment checking, QCP needs to find a witness for p_prev. For the entry case, p_prev = tail_node_prev. For the return case, p_prev = 0.

Hmm, this is getting complex. Let me just try a different simpler invariant.

What about:
```
dlistrep_shape(*head, 0)
```

This says the whole list from head to tail is a dlistrep. It's very simple — covers all nodes from head to tail. But it doesn't track p's position. data_at(head, head_v) *
```
dllseg_shape(head_v, 0, p_prev, p) *
data_at(p->next, p_next) *
dlistrep_shape(p_next, p)
```

Right side (postcondition):
```
data_at(head_pre, head_node) *
data_at(tail_pre, tail_node) *
dlistrep_shape(p, 0)
```

We need to:
1. Prove data_at(head_pre, head_node): head should equal head_pre (stack address = symbolic address), and head_v = head_node (since p = head_v and p = head_node at exit). 
2. Prove data_at(tail_pre, tail_node): This is NOT in the invariant left side.
3. Prove dlistrep_shape(p, 0): From the invariant, we have dllseg_shape(p, 0, p_prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p_next, p). We need to combine these into dlistrep_shape(p, 0).

For point 3: dllseg_shape(p, 0, p_prev, p) = dllseg_shape(head_node, 0, p_prev, head_node). For this to be the base case (emp), we need head_node == head_node (true) AND 0 == p_prev. So p_prev must be 0.

But where does p_prev come from? It's from evaluating `p->prev` = `head_node->prev`. In a well-formed DLL, head_node->prev = 0. So p_prev = 0 and dllseg is emp.

Then we have data_at(p->prev, 0) = data_at(head_node->prev, 0), data_at(p->next, p_next), and dlistrep_shape(p_next, head_node).

Can we fold: data_at(head_node->next, p_next) * data_at(head_node->prev, 0) * dlistrep_shape(p_next, head_node) → dlistrep_shape(head_node, 0)?

Yes, this is the definition of dlistrep_shape! It should work. But maybe QCP can't do this fold because the two data_at come from different sources (field expressions created by the invariant, not from strategy unfolding).

For point 2: data_at(tail_pre, tail_node) is missing. This is likely the cause of return_wit_1 failure.

So the fix is: add `*tail` to the invariant to preserve it. How?

The standard strategy for dlistrep_shape is:
- Rule 5: unfold dlistrep_shape(l, p) when l != 0 → data_at * data_at * dlistrep_shape
- Rule 6: fold data_at * data_at * dlistrep_shape → dlistrep_shape when the pieces match

So rule 6 should fold: data_at(l->next, t) * data_at(l->prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(t, l) → dlistrep_shape(l, p).

On the LHS after unfolding: data_at(tail_node->next, y) * data_at(tail_node->prev, tail_node_prev) * dlistrep_shape(y, tail_node).

To fold back to dlistrep_shape(tail_node, tail_node_prev):
- l = tail_node, t = y, p = tail_node_prev

These match the unfold products exactly. So rule 6 should fold them back. ✓

This is exactly what SKILL.md fix strategy 2 warns about: "A field expression in SEP or PROP creates an implicit data_at for that field. If a SEP predicate already encodes the same field, the field expression creates a duplicate."

Hmm wait, I need to be more precise. When QCP processes `dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev)`:
- arg[0] = p (a program variable, evaluated to its current value)
- arg[1] = p->prev (a C field expression, which QCP resolves to a value by reading p->prev)

For arg[1], QCP creates `data_at(&(p->prev), p->prev_val)` where p->prev_val is the value read from p->prev. This data_at gives QCP permission to read p->prev.

But `dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev)` also "owns" p's prev field (through the predicate semantics). When strategies unfold dlistrep at p, they produce `data_at(p->prev, p->prev_val)` — which is the SAME field!

But part 1 uses p->prev as the last node (yp), and part 2 uses p->prev as the start's prev (xp). Both reference p->prev. Are they the same usage?

In dllseg_shape(x, xp, yp, y):
- xp: prev pointer of start node → x->prev = xp
- yp: last node in segment → yp->next = y

Part 1: dllseg_shape(head_node, 0, p->prev, p). x = head_node, xp = 0, yp = p->prev, y = p. So p->prev is the last node IN the segment, with p->prev->next = p.

Part 2: dllseg_shape(p, p->prev, tail_node_prev, tail_node). x = p, xp = p->prev, yp = tail_node_prev, y = tail_node. So p->prev is the prev pointer of start node p. i.e., p->prev = p->prev (trivially). Wait, xp is used as p->prev, which means QCP evaluates p->prev to get a pointer value, and that becomes xp (the prev of the start node).

So in part 1, p->prev is used as a NODE (yp). In part 2, p->prev is used as a VALUE (xp — the prev of start node p). Both involve evaluating p->prev. In part 1, p->prev is an argument to dllseg that positions the last node. In part 2, p->prev is the value that establishes p->prev = p->prev (trivial identity).

Does using p->prev as a node in part 1 create data_at for p->prev? In dllseg_shape(x, xp, yp, y), yp is just a parameter — it identifies which node is the last one. When dllseg unfolds from the left, it doesn't create data_at for yp directly. Instead, the unfolding reaches yp eventually and then stops.

Does part 2's use of p->prev as xp create data_at for p->prev? xp is p->prev = the value of p's prev field. When dllseg_shape(p, p->prev, ...) unfolds from the start (p), it creates data_at(p->next, z) * data_at(p->prev, p->prev) * dllseg_shape(z, p, ...). Wait, that's data_at(p->prev, p->prev) — the VALUE of p->prev is p->prev (trivially). This IS a data_at for p->prev.

So yes, p->prev creates data_at in part 2 during unfolding. And part 1 doesn't directly create data_at for p->prev during unfolding (it's the rightmost node, reached at the end of unfolding). But when both are unfolded, there might be a data_at from part 2 that duplicates with something from part 1.

New: Part 1 (head to p_prev_new just before p_prev) + data_at(p_prev) [Part 2 unfolded]

Let me look at this from yet another angle. Maybe the issue is simpler than I think. Let me look at the overall entail_wit_2 for attempt 2:

```
forall (head_v_2: Z) (head: Z) (p_prev_2: Z) (p: Z) (y: Z),
  [| (p <> head_v_2) |] 
  && [| (p <> 0) |]
  && data_at(p->prev, p_prev_2)
  ** dlistrep_shape(y, p)
  ** data_at(p->next, y)
  ** data_at(head, head_v_2)
  ** dllseg_shape(head_v_2, 0, p_prev_2, p)
|--
  EX (head_v: Z) (p_prev: Z),
  [| (p_prev_2 <> 0) |]
  && data_at(head, head_v)
  ** dllseg_shape(head_v, 0, p_prev, p_prev_2)
  ** dlistrep_shape(p_prev_2, p_prev)
```

Wait, the LHS has p as the OLD p value. After `p = p->prev`, the NEW p is p_prev_2 (the value read from p->prev). The LHS represents the state after the assignment: p is the old p, but the dllseg shows `dllseg_shape(head_v_2, 0, p_prev_2, p)`.

Hmm, the LHS has `data_at(p->prev, p_prev_2)`. This means p->prev = p_prev_2. And the dllseg has `dllseg_shape(head_v_2, 0, p_prev_2, p)`. This segment goes from head to p_prev_2, not including p. So p_prev_2 is the node before p.

The RHS invariant should be about the NEW p = p_prev_2:
`dllseg_shape(head_v, 0, p_prev, p_prev_2) * dlistrep_shape(p_prev_2, p_prev)`.

The LHS has `dllseg_shape(head_v_2, 0, p_prev_2, p)`. To get `dllseg_shape(head_v, 0, p_prev, p_prev_2)`, we need to contract the segment by removing the last node (p_prev_2). But the segment ends at p_prev_2 (inclusive), with endpoint p. Contracting from the right means removing the last node p_prev_2 from the segment. The LHS doesn't have a direct rule for this.

The SKILL.md fix strategy 3 suggests using `p->prev` as the last node in dllseg: `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p->prev, p)`. When p changes to p->prev, the field expression re-evaluates, automatically adjusting the segment.


Both predicates are folded. The field expression p->prev appears as an argument. When QCP creates the goal, it translates these to:

- `dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev)`

Actually, in separation logic, when we have `{P} C {Q}`, P is the precondition, Q is the postcondition. The loop invariant I is checked at the loop head. The standard rule is:
```
{P} loop {Q} if:
  P => I              (entry)
  I && cond => body preserves I   (preservation)  
  I && !cond => Q     (exit)
```

For the invariant to work, I only needs to IMPLY Q (combined with !cond). If Q needs `*tail == tail_node`, then I must imply this. But I doesn't need to include the spatial resource for *tail — it just needs to imply it.

Hmm, but in separation logic, implication works differently. If the spatial resource for *tail is in P but not in I, then I |-- Q (exit) might fail because the spatial resource was "dropped" by the invariant.

Actually, the standard loop rule in separation logic is:
```
{P} while (B) C {Q} if exists I such that:
  P |-- I                          (inv holds initially)
  {I && B} C {I}                  (inv preserved)
  I && !B |-- Q                   (inv + exit condition implies post)
```

Where `P |-- I` means P semantically implies I. And the third condition means I *must* provide enough resources to satisfy Q (when combined with !B).

So if Q needs data_at(tail_pre, tail_node), then I must provide it (or logically imply it). If I doesn't contain this data_at, the exit entailment fails.

This confirms that *tail MUST be in the invariant if the postcondition needs it.

Wait, `p->prev` in `dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev)` creates data_at for p->prev. The exists `p_prev` in dllseg_shape is a pure value, not a field expression. These are DIFFERENT uses:
- dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev): p->prev is evaluated to get the prev pointer value. Creates data_at(p->prev, ...).
- dllseg_shape(*head, 0, p_prev, p): p_prev is the last node in segment. No data_at created for p_prev itself.

So there's no dup! One predicate uses the field expression, the other uses an exists variable.

For entry (p = tail_node, p->prev = tail_node_prev):
- dllseg_shape(head_node, 0, p_prev, tail_node): choose p_prev = tail_node_prev → matches precondition ✓
- dlistrep_shape(tail_node, tail_node->prev) = dlistrep_shape(tail_node, tail_node_prev) ✓
- data_at(*tail, struct list*, tail_node) ✓

For exit (p = head_node, p->prev = 0):
- dllseg_shape(head_node, 0, p_prev, head_node): for this to be emp, need p_prev = 0
- dlistrep_shape(head_node, head_node->prev) = dlistrep_shape(head_node, 0) ✓

For preservation: after p = p->prev (new p = old_p->prev = p_prev_2):
- Old invariant: dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, old_p) * dlistrep_shape(old_p, old_p->prev)
- After p = p->prev: new p = p_prev_2. Old_p->prev = p_prev_2.
- dlistrep_shape(old_p, p_prev_2) can be unfolded: data_at(old_p->next, ...) * data_at(old_p->prev, p_prev_2) * dlistrep_shape(old_p->next, old_p)
- New invariant should be: dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev_new, new_p) * dlistrep_shape(new_p, new_p->prev)
  = dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev_new, p_prev_2) * dlistrep_shape(p_prev_2, p_prev_2->prev)

From the unfolded old invariant, we have data_at(old_p->prev, p_prev_2) and dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, old_p). The dllseg has old_p as the endpoint (NOT in segment). The last node in the segment is p_prev, and p_prev->next = old_p.

We need to contract the dllseg: remove the node p_prev_2 from consideration. But p_prev_2 = old_p->prev, which is NOT in the dllseg (it's the value stored in old_p->prev). The last node IN the segment is p_prev, which is different from p_prev_2.

Hmm, actually p is old_p. old_p->prev = p_prev_2. And the dllseg has: dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, old_p). The last node in the segment is p_prev. Hmm, in the invariant `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, p)`, p_prev is existentially quantified. For the entry, p_prev = tail_node_prev. The LHS segment goes from head to p_prev_2, endpoint = p (old p). The RHS segment goes from head to p_prev, endpoint = p_prev_2 (new p).

After p = p->prev (new p = old p->prev):

These re-evaluate automatically. No need to contract dllseg from the right — the new invariant is independently computed from the new p value. ✓

Wait, no. The standard rule is:
1. Entry: exec code before loop, then check invariant (before first condition check)
2. Preservation: assume invariant + condition, exec body, check invariant (before next condition check)
3. Exit: assume invariant + NOT condition, check postcondition

So at exit, the invariant is assumed true with `!(p != *head)`, i.e., `p == *head`. The invariant must be consistent with `p == *head`.

- After loop: invariant && !cond → postcondition

- Precondition: dllseg + dlistrep, where the split is at p (between traversed and untraversed)
- Invariant: dllseg(head, 0, p_prev, p) * dlistrep(p, p_prev) for forward traversal
  - Forward traversal has p advancing forward: p = p->next
  - After p advances, p_prev (old p) is now inside dllseg (which UNFOLDS from the LEFT and EXTENDS from the RIGHT — this IS supported)
- Fix for backward: dllseg(head, 0, p->prev, p) * dlistrep(p->next, p)
  - Backward traversal has p retreating backward: p = p->prev
  - After p retreats, the boundary shifts — both field expressions auto-evaluate

The difference: in forward traversal, the invariant naturally handles preservation (unfold from left, extend to right). In backward, it's trickier.

For backward with the exists invariant: after p = p->prev, the segment needs to CONTRACT from the RIGHT (remove one node). This is NOT supported.

For backward with field expressions: both sides auto-evaluate, so preservation works. But entry/exit need careful field expression choices.

In the SKILL.md fix: `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p->prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p->next, p)` — the dlistrep starts at p->next, not p. The invariant `dllseg_shape(*head, 0, p->prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p->next, p)`:
- dllseg covers head to p->prev (with p->prev->next = p)
- dlistrep covers p->next to end
- p itself is NOT in either predicate, but its fields (prev and next) are referenced in the arguments

So p (the boundary node) is "split" between the two predicates. Its prev field is the last node in dllseg, and its next field is the start of dlistrep. The ownership of p's fields comes from the field expressions, not from a shape predicate.

At entry (p = tail_node):
- dllseg covers head to tail_node->prev = tail_node_prev ✓
- dlistrep covers tail_node->next = 0 to end → emp

- dllseg covers same ✓
- dlistrep covers tail_node's fields (data_at + rest)

The total heap is the same — both cover all nodes and their fields. But the representation differs! The precondition uses a SHAPE PREDICATE (dlistrep) for tail_node, while the invariant uses IMPLICIT data_at from field expressions.

For entail_wit_1: LHS has dlistrep_shape(tail_node, tail_node_prev) (a shape predicate). RHS has data_at(tail_node->prev, tail_node_prev) + data_at(tail_node->next, 0) (individual data_at from field expressions).

For the entailment to work, strategies need to UNFOLD the LHS dlistrep_shape to produce individual data_at, then match them with the RHS's field expression data_at.

Unfolding dlistrep_shape(tail_node, tail_node_prev): data_at(tail_node->next, 0) * data_at(tail_node->prev, tail_node_prev) * dlistrep_shape(0, tail_node). And dlistrep_shape(0, tail_node) = emp.

So: data_at(tail_node->next, 0) * data_at(tail_node->prev, tail_node_prev).

RHS: data_at(tail_node->prev, tail_node_prev) * data_at(tail_node->next, 0) [order might differ].

These are the same! So the entailment SHOULD work. But it's Admitted, meaning the strategies can't prove it.

This is a strategy limitation, not a conceptual error. The invariant IS correct — the strategies just can't verify it automatically.

```c
struct list *iter_back_2(struct list **head, struct list **tail)
/*@ With head_node tail_node tail_node_prev
    Require *head == head_node && *tail == tail_node && head_node != 0 && tail_node != 0 && dllseg_shape(head_node,0,tail_node_prev,tail_node) * dlistrep_shape(tail_node, tail_node_prev)
```

## Summary

The task was to infer a loop invariant for `iter_back_2.c`, a DLL backward traversal loop (`p = p->prev`), using the SKILL.md workflow.

### What was done:
1. **Step 1: Symbolic Execution & State Collection** - Successfully unrolled the loop, ran symexec, and parsed 3 recording points (RP_pre_loop, RP_iter_2, RP_iter_3).
2. **Step 2: Invariant Inference** - Analyzed the RPs showing backward traversal. The invariant structure needed to split the DLL at `p`:
   - `dllseg_shape(*head, 0, p->prev, p)` covers head to node before p
   - `dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev)` or `dlistrep_shape(p->next, p)` covers rest
3. **Step 3: QCP Verification** - Tested 6 candidate invariants. All failed with 2-3 Admitted Coq proof obligations.

### Key Findings:
- The structurally correct invariant for backward DLL traversal is per SKILL.md fix strategy 3: `dllseg_shape(*head, 0, p->prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p->next, p)`
- This invariant correctly handles entry (matches precondition when p=tail_node) and exit (implies `dlistrep_shape(head_node, 0)` when p=head_node) in theory
- However, QCP's strategies cannot automatically prove the entailment witnesses for this specific precondition structure (where the precondition splits at `tail_node_prev/tail_node` rather than at `p->prev/p->next`)
- The exists-based alternative (`dllseg_shape(*head, 0, p_prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev)`) fails preservation because strategies cannot contract dllseg from the right during backward traversal

### Result: `QCP_VERIFIED: NO`
Final invariant: `/*@ Inv Assert p != 0 && dllseg_shape(*head, 0, p->prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p->next, p) */`
